SkinHealth Systems (SKIN) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Consumables returned to growth as a U.S. rental program takes shape.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

SkinHealth Systems Inc. (SKIN +28.55%) reported a narrowing of revenue declines and a return to growth for its global consumables segment during the third quarter. Management focused on stabilizing the Hydrafacial core through improved commercial execution and the introduction of a U.S. rental program to address capital expenditure barriers for providers. The company achieved its third consecutive quarter of adjusted gross margin above 70% while retiring its near-term debt maturity using cash on hand. Strategic initiatives emphasized increasing the productivity of the existing installed base and expanding the product portfolio into complementary aesthetic categories like microneedling.

Operator: This call is being recorded on Thursday, November 5th, 2026. I would now like to turn the conference over to Norberto Aja, Investor Relations.

Norberto Aja: Thank you, operator, and good afternoon, everyone. Thank you for joining SkinHealth Systems' third quarter 2026 conference call. We released our results earlier this afternoon, which can be found on our corporate website at skinhealthsystems.com. Joining me on the call today is SkinHealth Systems President and Chief Executive Officer, Pedro Malha, along with our Chief Financial Officer, Mike Monahan. Before we begin, I want to remind everyone of the company's safe harbor language. Management may make forward-looking statements within the meaning of Section 27A of the Securities Act of 1933, as amended, and Section 21E of the Securities Exchange Act of 1934, as amended, including guidance and underlying assumptions.

Forward-looking statements are based on current expectations and beliefs and involve risks and uncertainties that could cause actual results to differ materially. Listeners are cautioned not to place undue reliance on any forward-looking statements. For a further discussion of risks related to our business, please refer to the risk factors contained in the company's filings with the Securities and Exchange Commission. In addition, this call will present non-GAAP financial measures. Management uses adjusted EBITDA and other non-GAAP measures as supplemental measures to evaluate operating performance and to facilitate period-to-period comparisons. These non-GAAP measures should be considered in addition to, and not as a substitute for, financial information prepared in accordance with GAAP.

A reconciliation of these non-GAAP financial measures to the most comparable GAAP measure is available in the earnings press release, which was furnished to the SEC, as well as available on our website. Following management's prepared remarks, we will open the call for a question-and-answer session. With that, I would now like to turn the call over to our President and CEO, Pedro Malha. Please go ahead, Pedro.

Pedro Malha: Thank you, Norberto, and good afternoon, everybody. Let me start with a central message from this quarter. We are making progress on the things that matter the most, stabilizing the Hydrafacial business and improving the economics of the company. At the same time, we are developing new opportunities for growth. To be clear, the work isn't done. Equipment sales remain challenging, particularly in EMEA, and utilization in our installed base is still below where it should be. But we are making good progress against our plan, and the third quarter results provide tangible evidence that we are operating from a stronger foundation than a year ago.

Our third quarter revenue was $69.2 million, towards the upper end of our $65 million-$70 million guidance range, meaning that our quarterly revenue declined 2.1% year-over-year, which is a meaningful improvement from the declines of 7.8% in the second quarter and 6.7% in the first quarter. Importantly, global consumables returned to growth, up 1.2% on a year-over-year comparison, while equipment remained the primary headwind. The rate of the overall revenue decline has moderated significantly. At the same time, the earnings profile of the business continues to improve. On a GAAP basis, gross margin was 68.2%, operating income was approximately $5 million, and net loss was approximately $1 million.

We delivered positive GAAP operating income for the second consecutive quarter, and adjusted gross margin was 71.8%, our third consecutive quarter above 70%. Adjusted EBITDA was $13.4 million. Some of our quarterly bottom line outperformance benefits from one-time item and expense timing, but we are also seeing the benefits of better margins, tighter cost management, and a more efficient operating model. The priority now is to sustain those improvements while returning revenue to growth. Looking across our regions, we are seeing encouraging signs in the Americas, which grew 1.4%, with consumables growing 4.3% on a year-over-year comparison.

EMEA remained challenged, with revenues down approximately 12% on a year-over-year comparison, reflecting pressure in delivery systems, particularly in Germany, where execution has been below expectations. To address this, we have strengthened regional leadership, and we are taking actions to improve field execution and customer engagement. In APAC, revenue declined approximately 4% on a year-over-year comparison, primarily reflecting the transition in Australia and New Zealand to a distributor model. While our performance varies across markets, we know where the pressure is, and we are taking actions to address it. Let me turn now to our three strategic priorities and the progress we are making in building a stronger, more sustainable growth business.

Our first priority is strengthen the core Hydrafacial franchise. We have a fantastic global brand, a deep customer relationship, but we need to translate those advantages into better commercial execution. Over the past year, we organized our commercial teams, strengthened accountability, and invested in better training, processes, and selling tools. We are also taking a more targeted approach to customers, with a great emphasis on Hydrafacial clinical differentiators and the economic value it creates for providers. Additionally, we are expanding our relationships with corporate and multi-location accounts, where we see opportunities to broaden our installed base and increase treatment volumes.

During the quarter, we introduced a U.S. rental program to address one of the key barriers to equipment adoption, the upfront capital investment. It is still early, but the objective is to make Hydrafacial more accessible and bring more providers into the Hydrafacial ecosystem and support long-term relationships with them. We are also advancing our next-generation Hydrafacial platform, currently targeted for 2028, with the goal of improving clinical outcomes, provider workflow, and overall treatment experience. On competition, we have not seen a meaningful change in intensity from what we discussed last quarter. Our strategy is not simply to compete on price. It is to strengthen our differentiation, improve commercial execution, and deliver greater value to our providers.

Our second strategic priority is getting more value from the Hydrafacial installed base. Throughout the years, we have built a substantial global network of providers, and that represents one of our biggest competitive advantage. But placing a system is only the beginning of the customer relationship. The real opportunity lies in helping providers perform more treatments, increase utilization, and generate more recurring consumables revenue. We saw encouraging progress in the quarter, with global consumables growing 1.2% on a year-over-year comparison, and the Americas consumables returning to growth for the first time this year, supported by strong signature consumables performance, plus a Hydrafacial relaunch and other commercial initiatives.

But consumables revenues per systems remain below last year, and we believe we can do more to improve productivity across the install base. That is an important part of our innovation strategy, bringing providers clinically differentiated treatments that address real consumer needs and create more reasons for consumers to come back. So in September, we introduced our treatment enhancers, the HydraGlow Pre-Extraction Peel and the Hydralock HA Biocellulose Mask. In October, we launched a new booster, HydraClear, for blemish-prone skin, which in clinical evaluation showed that a series of HydraClear treatments produce a 38% mean reduction in total blemishes.

So these launches are a good example of the direction we are taking with innovation, more targeted, clinical-supported products that address specific customer needs. Our HydraScalp relaunch is another good example of how we are working to expand how providers can use their existing Hydrafacial system in the scalp and in the hair health. Equally important is our work in helping providers turn this innovation into greater treatment volumes, and we are doing this with education programs like Hydrafacial Impact, which was built to help providers attract more customers and increase repeat visits and improve their treatment economics. The point is not simply to launch more products. It is to make the systems already in the market more productive.

If we execute well, we can create a meaningful recurring revenue opportunity without relying entirely on new equipment placements. Our third strategic priority is to build a broader skin health business. Our ambition is not to be limited to one product or one category. We want to participate in attractive, high-growth segments of the aesthetic market and offer providers complementary treatments and give consumers more reasons to engage with our brands. SkinStylus is a good example of that strategy in play. Microneedling is an attractive and growing segment that complements Hydrafacial, and SkinStylus gives us a strong platform to compete in that market, offering both microneedling and nano-channeling treatments. SkinStylus consumables grew 39% year-over-year this quarter.

Very recently, we expanded SkinStylus into six key European markets, leveraging our existing international commercial infrastructure. We are also making good progress towards a planned introduction of another complementary device in 2027, with more details to come. But what is important strategically is that we believe we already have the customer relationships, the commercial infrastructure, and the clinical credibility that we can leverage those advantages across a broader portfolio. That is how we can build a stronger company, not by moving away from what made us successful, but by building on it. So let me bring all of this together. The revenue decline has narrowed significantly.

Overall, consumables have returned to growth, margins are substantially stronger, and we are making progress on the commercial and innovation initiatives that can drive the next phase of our business. We still have work to do on the equipment sales, utilization, and EMEA. But when I look at SkinHealth Systems, I see a much bigger opportunity than the one we are capturing today. We have a strong global brand, a substantial install base, a more efficient operating model, and an expanding portfolio. Our job now is to turn those advantages into sustainable growth through better execution in the core business, greater productivity from our install base, and a disciplined expansion into new categories.

That is exactly what we are focusing on delivering. With that, I'll just turn the call over to Mike.

Mike Monahan: Thank you, Pedro. We are pleased with our company's performance this quarter. Our cross-functional teams were able to deliver on our revenue commitments, and we overperformed on driving adjusted EBITDA. The team was able to do this while continuing to invest in future growth initiatives. We're grateful for the hard work that everyone in this organization put into delivering. Thank you to the team. Total net sales in the third quarter were $69.2 million, down 2.1% compared with the prior year. This was primarily driven by decline in delivery system units sold year-over-year, partially offset by an increase in consumables revenue. Specifically, at the end of Q3, global active systems grew to 37,342.

We continue to grow our footprint and provider reach each quarter. Q3 delivery systems revenue was $18.8 million, down 9.9%. We placed 798 systems during the quarter compared with 875 in the prior year period, a decline of 8.8%. Average selling price was $23,509, down 1.2% year-over-year. Consumables revenue was $50.4 million, up 1.2% year-over-year, representing approximately 73% of total revenue. On a year-over-year comparison, Americas net sales were $48.9 million, up 1.4%, with consumables up 4.3%, partially offset by continued pressure on equipment. EMEA net sales were $14.2 million, down 11.7%, with delivery systems the largest source of pressure, and consumables down 6.6%.

APAC net sales were $6.1 million, down 4.2%, primarily reflecting the Australia and New Zealand distributor transition. GAAP gross margin was 68.2% compared with 64.6% in the prior year period. Adjusted gross margin was 71.8% compared with 68% in Q3 2025, an improvement of approximately 380 basis points, and our third consecutive quarter above 70%. There were several drivers of the year-over-year improvement. First, the prior year period included higher costs associated with Elite trade-in systems that did not repeat. As a reminder, we sold through our fair market value Elite systems that were acquired prior to 2024. Going forward, this inventory is no longer on our balance sheet.

Second, we recorded approximately $1 million of tariff refunds during the quarter, which contributed approximately 140 basis points to adjusted gross margin. This was a one-time benefit. Third, we continued to benefit from improved inventory management, including lower excess, obsolete, and scrap charges. Fourth, revenue mix benefited from a higher proportion of consumables as our consolidated consumables revenue reached 73% for the quarter. For the fourth quarter, we expect adjusted gross margin to moderate from third quarter levels as equipment becomes a larger percentage of the mix and the tariff benefit does not repeat. GAAP operating expenses were approximately $42.2 million compared with $51.9 million in the prior year period.

Adjusted operating expenses were approximately $36.3 million compared with $39.1 million. The year-over-year improvement reflects the benefit of our APAC go-to-market changes, lower professional fees, and lower commissions, partially offset by increased marketing and event spending. Approximately $1.3 million of planned third quarter expense also shifted into the fourth quarter, roughly $600,000 of marketing, $600,000 of research and development, and $100,000 of G&A. As a result, we are projecting the fourth quarter run rate to be higher than the third quarter expense level. Research and development is expected to increase meaningfully in the fourth quarter as our device development programs, clinical studies, and other innovation initiatives advance.

On a GAAP basis, income from operations was approximately $5 million compared with an operating loss of $6.2 million in the prior year quarter. This is our second consecutive quarter of positive GAAP operating income. Net loss was approximately $0.9 million. Adjusted EBITDA was $13.4 million, or 19.3% of revenue, compared with $8.9 million, or 12.6% in the prior year period, an increase of approximately 50%. Cash flow from operations was approximately $11.3 million in the third quarter, and capital expenditure was approximately $1.1 million, resulting in free cash flow of approximately $10.2 million. On September 30th, we repaid the remaining $103.2 million principal amount of our 1.25% convertible senior notes due in 2026.

Together with approximately $645,000 of accrued and unpaid interest, the related indenture was satisfied and discharged. This completed the retirement of the 2026 notes and delivered on the plan we communicated earlier this year to address the maturity with cash on hand. Following the repayment, we ended the quarter with approximately $112.4 million in cash and cash equivalents. We believe our liquidity position provides sufficient flexibility to operate and invest in the business. We have approximately $250 million in convertible debt remaining that matures in Q4 of 2028. We expect to address this in 2027, and we will evaluate all alternatives thoughtfully as we move forward. Turning to our outlook.

Based on third quarter results and our current expectations for the balance of the year, we are updating our full year 2026 guidance. We now expect net sales of $281 million-$287 million, compared with our previous guidance of $280 million-$290 million. We now expect adjusted EBITDA of $48 million-$50 million, compared with our previous guidance of $39 million-$46 million. Our fourth quarter assumptions reflect continued pressure on equipment and further work to improve EMEA revenue performance. We expect adjusted gross margin to moderate sequentially, as equipment represents a greater percentage of the mix and the one-time tariff benefit does not repeat. Finally, the guidance reflects increased operating expenses quarter-over-quarter.

This is primarily driven by increased sales and marketing expenses due to normal seasonal commission increases tied to revenue, along with some timing of marketing expenses that are now expected to impact the fourth quarter. Our research and development expenses are projected to increase meaningfully from the third quarter, as a number of our 2027 and 2028 projects are beginning to ramp. Finally, we are projecting increased G&A expenses, primarily due to increased professional fees, more normalized bad debt expense, and some timing of legal expenses. With that, I will turn the call back to Pedro Malha.

Pedro Malha: Thanks, Mike. Let me close with a few thoughts. We are not simply waiting for the equipment market to recover. We are taking action to build a stronger, more profitable business with multiple opportunities for growth. This quarter demonstrates that we are making progress. Revenue is stabilizing, consumables on the overall basis have returned to growth, and the economics of the business continue to improve. At the same time, we are investing in the things that will drive our future, strengthen our Hydrafacial franchise, getting more value from our install base, and building a broader portfolio of products and treatments.

Like Mike mentioned, we also retired our near-term 2026 notes upon maturity as planned, while maintaining a meaningful liquidity to continue investing in our business. While there is still work ahead, we believe we are operating from a much stronger foundation than we were at the beginning of the year. We also believe that we have the brand, the infrastructure, the relationships, and the innovation pipeline to build a substantially better and more diversified business. Our focus now is on execution, translating those advantages into consistent revenue growth, sustained profitability, and long-term value for our shareholders. Operator, you can now open the line for questions.

Operator: Thank you. Ladies and gentlemen, we will now begin our question-and-answer session. Should you have a question, please press star followed by the one on your touchtone phone. You will hear a prompt that your hand has been raised. Should you wish to decline from the polling process, please press the star followed by the two. If you are using the speakerphone, please lift the handset first before pressing any keys. We have our first question from Olivia Tong with Raymond James.

Olivia Tong: Great, thanks. Good afternoon. Two questions from me. First, can you talk about the growth in consumables? First, do you think you have turned the corner and we can expect continued growth from here, or was there anything particularly notable this quarter that does not repeat? Then what drove the growth? Was it more treatments, more boosters, pricing, other factors there? Secondly, can you give us a sense on the delivery on the system side, what churn looks like? It is a bit difficult to assess now that you have got the leasing program in place, so would love to understand that as well. Thank you so much.

Pedro Malha: Sure, Olivia. On the consumables, let me bring you back. Global consumables increased 1.2% in this quarter, and actually the Americas consumables grew 4.2%. Good numbers. These are encouraging signals in terms of performance. To support that, the signature consumables are also performing better. Together with the HydraScalp relaunch, that basically added more treatments. The Americas, specifically the U.S., is looking good, and this is a direct result of the execution work, the commercial work, the retraining of the sales force, the marketing resegmentation, remessaging. All that work is starting to pay out in the U.S. We feel also encouraged that in the quarter, we launched HydraClear, a very promising booster, and also the non-medical channel is supporting that growth.

Overall, I think things are starting to shape up on the consumables side, specifically in our most important market, which is the U.S. In terms of the system churn, Mike, do you want to take that one now?

Mike Monahan: Sure. Hi, Olivia. We had positive net churn of 59 units, if you roll it forward. This was primarily driven in the Americas, as we saw a lower fall-off of devices and some consumers repurchasing that were previously dormant. We're encouraged by some of the improvements that we've made, specifically around with our outreach to past dormant account. We're seeing some of them come back.

Operator: We have our next question from Sydney Wagner with Jefferies.

Sydney Wagner: Hi. Thanks for taking our question. As wellness becomes a larger part of the med spa offering, are hair health concerns, including those associated with GLP-1 use and the side effects from that, bringing new consumers to the ecosystem? If we think about HydraScalp and the opportunity there, are you primarily seeing deepened engagement with existing consumers, or are you seeing incremental consumers coming in through that offering? On EMEA, last quarter you cited personnel gaps and distributor order timing as temporary pressures, and they were expected to improve in the second half. Just curious how those issues are running now and what needs to change for that region to improve further. Thank you.

Pedro Malha: Sure, Sydney. In terms of scalp health, this is a segment that is getting a lot of attention and a lot of traction, independently of what's driving. Obviously, GLP-1s could be a good contributor for that renewed interest in scalp health. HydraScalp for us, and HydraScalp with Keravive, is a particularly good example of the strategy that we put in place of getting more value out of our installed base.

The whole premise of this is that we want to extend the use of our existing Hydrafacial system beyond just the facial treatments, and going to the scalp and going to hair health, which, as I mentioned in the beginning, is getting some good traction as a segment overall in the market, and getting a lot of growing consumer interest. Basically, what is so appealing for us in terms of this treatment, that it offers and combines basically an in-office treatment with a home spray made out of peptides, and basically creates another opportunity for providers.

As I keep mentioning the first answer to the first question, we saw this being a good contributor to the numbers of the consumables that we have seen in the U.S., in terms of the quarter. So now the opportunity is to expand that provider adoption of HydraScalp with Keravive, and just build that recurring treatment volume on top of it. In terms of EMEA, yes, definitely a point of pressure. It has been a point of pressure for our business for some quarters now. We have taken decisive action in terms of leadership and organization design to better address the needs and the dynamics of the EMEA market in the past quarter.

I feel very encouraged and very positive with those changes, and the tools and the processes that we are bringing in place. I feel optimistic that EMEA will start providing better results in the future.

Mike Monahan: The only thing I would add, Sydney, on the numbers. We did see some improvement, even though in EMEA, we were down 19% year-over-year, as you might remember, in Q2. That improved, still down, but was down 11.7% in Q3. To Pedro's point, even though the changes came throughout the summer, we are starting to see some improvement there.

Operator: Our next question comes from Joe Federico with Stifel.

Joe Federico: Hey, everyone. Thanks for taking the question. Maybe just to start a little high level, delivery systems in the quarter placements were actually pretty impressive relative to our estimate. I know it's now early days for the U.S. rental program, but maybe just any early learnings you're seeing from that program. Can you give us a bit of color on the economics of those placements from the system side? I can have a quick follow-up. Thanks.

Pedro Malha: Yeah, Joe, I appreciate the comment. In terms of delivery systems, yes, the numbers were, I wouldn't call it good, but they were less severe than they were in the last quarter. But definitely, and I can be straightforward with this, we're still below where we needed to be. What we're seeing in the market is that capital decisions, they still remain a barrier. The sales cycles are longer, and the competition is there. As I mentioned before, EMEA is the largest source of pressure that we have right now. But we are focused on making the product easier to access and the value proposition easier to sell.

I do believe that devices eventually will return to growth, but we have to improve conversion. We have to bring more discipline to the channel's coverage. We have to reduce the adoption friction and basically give customers and providers a compelling reason to invest. The rental program and I will also add to this, the corporate account strategy that we have now in place work on access today. The next generation platform that is slated for 2028, it will be the long-term innovation driver. In terms of the U.S. rental program specifically, that program was introduced during the quarter, so it's still too early to kind of attribute a meaningful share of Q3 performance to it.

But it basically was, as I mentioned before, it was designed to remove the upfront capital hurdle for the qualified providers. The process that we are going through right now is that we are evaluating the lead quality, we're evaluating the conversion, we're evaluating the provider adoption, and importantly, the treatment activity after every placement. Our expectation is that this program will build contribution over time as it scales in the U.S. right now.

Mike Monahan: Joe, I would just add on the economics, to follow up on that piece. The way the rental program works is our sales team is able to identify the sale, and if the end provider opts to enter into the rental program, we sell the unit to our financing partner, who then administrates the program. For us, the revenue is booked upfront upon sale, very similarly to the way we do it when we go through our normal sale process. For the rental program, it has a slightly lower gross margin, as our financing partner captures some of the economics for administrating the program and taking on the default risk.

It works in the same way as if we sell directly to the end provider.

Joe Federico: Very helpful. I'll keep it to one. Thanks for the color.

Operator: We have our next question from Navann Ty with BNP Paribas.

Navann Ty: Hi, good afternoon. Thanks for taking my question. I wonder if you can comment, maybe some early thoughts on how growth and margins could trend next year with different factors like the extension of the corporate multi-accounts, the rental program, and any other push and pull. Thank you.

Mike Monahan: Yeah, I mean, we're not commenting on 2027, Navann, right now. What we can comment in terms of overall expectations, is we're launching a number of initiatives next year. I would expect our plan is, and we'll give more color when we get to our next call, is that as we start to roll out these initiatives during the latter part of the first half of the year, you'll start to see the benefits, not just from some of the programs that we started investing in this year, but also from some of the new device and partnerships that Pedro had mentioned earlier that you'll see us start to announce as we get further into 2027.

Operator: Our next question is from Jonna Kim with TD Cowen.

Jonna Kim: Thank you for taking my question. The first one, just curious on what are your key sales and marketing initiatives that you're implementing to drive better system sales going forward? Any color will be helpful there. Then just on the end consumer itself, how is the health of the consumer looking? Are you seeing any sort of delay in treatment, just given where the macro backdrop is? Thank you.

Pedro Malha: Sure, Jonna. In terms of the changes and the work that we're doing in terms of sales force excellence and corporate accounts within the commercial organization, we have strengthened the leadership and invested in more consistent training. The bigger change is basically moving from a relationship-led model to a more deliberative demand generation and conversion model. That includes better account segmentation, better value-based selling, very disciplined follow-up from the sales force down in the field, very strong oversight from management over the health and the flow of the pipeline. All of that have been initiatives and a great focus of our commercial team and commercial leadership in the past quarter.

An example of programs that we drove in the quarter, one of those was the Hydrafacial Impact. Basically, the concept of the thesis is that innovation only works when providers know how to sell it, basically, and know how to perform and bring customers back. So, the Hydrafacial Impact program is really about helping our providers build better business and improving the treatment protocols and the treatment economics. It's very straightforward. Basically, it's when providers are more successful with Hydrafacial, they perform more treatments and utilization improves. Another work that we've been doing, and a lot of organizational changes as well, is around corporate accounts.

Here, we are taking just a more targeted approach to these type of customers, because basically they can provide scale. They can provide scale in placements. They provide scale in provider coverage and recurrent treatments when implemented well. There's a lot of work been done in that organization, and we are very optimistic about the outcomes of that work.

Mike Monahan: The only thing I would add to that is we're seeing some encouraging signs in the Americas on overall revenue per system that we saw in Q3, where we still have some pressure, but we've seen that decline to less than 1%. While we're a B2B business, it doesn't tell us exactly what's happening at the end consumer. That gives us some insight that we're seeing some health in terms of utilization, as Pedro mentioned, for the demand for our consumables, which is encouraging.

Pedro Malha: I will just end by addressing the question around the market and the consumer sentiment in terms of dynamics. Consumers are still interested in outcomes. That is still clear in the market. As we keep mentioning in the prior calls, their dollars are spread across more treatment and providers, and that is why we as an organization have to work harder to be the preferred treatment and help providers kind of monetize it better. This is why utilization, differentiation, and consumer experience matters in our business.

Also, the cost of capital is delaying equipment decision, and that is why the response from us has to be better provider economics, more flexible access, like we kept discussing here, the rental program, and treatments need to drive repeat visits. That is why we are so focused in having our booster and treatment enhancers and extender strategy.

Operator: Our next question is from Bruce Jackson with StoneX.

Bruce Jackson: Hi, thanks for taking the questions. On new products, you said you are going to be increasing R&D here. Is it going to be going up sequentially and year-over-year? You mentioned the new platform that you are working on. Is there anything else in the new product development that you are working on?

Pedro Malha: So, in terms of the new devices, the program continues to progress towards the planned 2027 launch. Again, this is not a Hydrafacial device. We are not going to give product specifics or economics today or commit to a launch. The commitment is 2027. But the strategic logic here is to add another complementary treatment category through an infrastructure that we already have. Again, we will provide more details when the product and the commercial plans are ready. In terms of the next-gen Hydrafacial, this is a very important long-term initiative for us, and that is targeted for 2028. But it is not part of the foundation of our near-term recovering plan, because that is 2028.

But we are designing and we are investing in a machine that will improve clinical outcomes, improve workflow, it will improve treatment experience, and it will create reasons for both the existing and the prospective providers to invest in a new device. We are not disclosing any product features this fall, and we will release more information as we see fit.

Bruce Jackson: Okay. Then a quick follow-up, just in terms of the product portfolio vitality. Do you have a rough idea of the percentage of the mix that the new products contribute to the sales, and is this something that could be expected to help accelerate revenue at some point in the future?

Pedro Malha: So, in terms of the strategy, absolutely. Basically, all these product launches are there to create more occasions to use the same system and serve in more ways the customers. The booster addresses specific skin concerns. The enhancer or the extender can personalize or broaden an existing treatment protocol. All of this supports premium treatments. All of this strategy supports repeat visits and higher consumables per treatment. That is the strategy. We are not going to give standalone revenue forecasts per product SKU, but the way to assess the success is how this is contributing to the recurring revenue over time.

New consumables require providers to adopt the protocols and consumables to incorporate them into the treatment routines that they have in these practices. If you look at SkinStylus, that is already generating growth. We are using SkinStylus to expand internationally. The other products are very early. We just launched them in their commercialization. I would not expect everything to show up all at once in one quarter. But the benefits of all these launches and the cadence of all these launches should build over time progressively as we see adoption in the markets increase.

I think the most important part of all of this is that we are working as a company to build several complementary growth drivers rather than just solely be focused on the recovering of equipment sales. That is the key work that we focus on.

Bruce Jackson: Okay. Thank you very much. That is it for me.

Operator: There are no further questions. Ladies and gentlemen, this concludes today's conference call. We thank you for your participation. You may now disconnect. Goodbye.

Solana’s Faster Finality Fails to Halt SOL’s Price Slide

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Home » Trendsetter News » Solana’s Faster Finality Fails to Halt SOL’s Price Slide

Solana’s Alpenglow upgrade reportedly cut transaction finality to roughly 400 milliseconds, but still can’t pick SOL’s price up.

Solana transactions reportedly reached finality in roughly 400 milliseconds after its Alpenglow upgrade, yet SOL fell after the switch. In the supplied video, a crypto analyst examines that disconnect: a faster network may improve its appeal to payment providers without immediately giving investors a reason to buy.

Fire Hustle places the upgrade’s launch on October 21 and describes SOL trading near $100 afterward. The YouTube video’s central argument is that technical progress remains secondary to broader market conditions and investment flows when it comes to short-term prices.

According to her, Alpenglow replaced Solana’s TowerBFT voting system, moving validator votes off-chain and reducing finality from about 12.8 seconds to roughly 400 milliseconds in the first day’s data. That remains above the team’s stated 150-millisecond target.

The transition required validators representing at least 82% of staked SOL to approve the new system before user transactions resumed. The analyst describes a brief pause rather than a reported outage, following months of test-network rehearsals.

There is also a software-diversity concern. The YouTube video episode says Firedancer validators could not participate in the switch and temporarily had to return to the main client, leaving the network more dependent on one implementation.

Moving votes off-chain also changes the transaction count displayed by block explorers. “Check what’s actually being counted before you panic,” she says, warning that lower headline transactions-per-second figures need not mean user activity has collapsed.

Fire Hustle reports 12 consecutive trading days of outflows from Solana ETFs and describes October as their worst month for withdrawals since launch. It also cites a roughly 12% weekly decline in open leveraged SOL positions on OKX.

Corporate buyers offered a counterpoint. According to Fire Hustle, DeFi Development Corp. purchased another 22,070 SOL during the decline, while Forward Industries added approximately 949,000 SOL in the quarter ending September.

On the chart, she watches support in the mid-$90s and an initial recovery zone around $110–$115. Those are conditional trading levels, not price forecasts: a weekly break below support could expose the $80s.

In a nutshell: faster settlement could support payment adoption, but sustained usage and a reversal in fund outflows would offer firmer evidence that the upgrade is translating into market demand.

These Risky Car Purchases Ended Up Being Way More Reliable For Our Readers Than Most People Would Assume

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Somehow, despite buying a used 2006 Ducati Monster with more than 20,000 miles on the odometer and having done no pre-purchase inspection, my bike ended up being pretty darn reliable. I had a feeling it would be based on the way the previous owner had cared for and modified it, and they'd clearly loved riding it, but other than replacing the front fork seals, I didn't have to do any work to it the entire time I lived in the Los Angeles metro area. Ducati may be the Ferrari of motorcycles, but it sure didn't feel like it based on running costs.

If you know motorcycles, you know that's because Ducati Monsters are pretty darn reliable. Non-motorcycle people tend to see the bright paint, wild styling, and Italian heritage and assume Monsters are in the shop just as often as an '80s Lamborghini. That got me thinking — surely, I can't be the only one who's owned vehicles that ended up being more reliable and cheaper to own than other people tend to assume.

So I asked for your examples, and boy, let me tell you. I did not need the encouragement some of your stories are giving me. Despite the horror stories, part of me wants to replace the Fiat with a BMW i8, and I'm going to have to get incredibly lucky if I'm going to pull that off without ruining my finances. But that doesn't mean I didn't enjoy the encouragement. Let's take a look at some of the most popular answers.

My 2014 Panamera 4 has been 100% rock-solid. I have done only routine maintenance except. Maintance isn't always cheap HOWEVER the car is one of the most reliable cars I have ever owned and now that I do my own oil changes (and might try brakes when they are due), the cost will drop even more.

Second has been the two Abarths I have owned (still have my 2014 that I autocross). The internet makes it seem like these cars will break-down as much as the old Fiat 124's but these cars have never let me down. In fact, working on my Abarth is extremely easy. There is a huge fanbase so finding videos or advice on modifying or DIY maintenance on these cars is easy. I push the Abarth all the time and it takes it.

Fun and reliable don't need to be an oxymoron.

I've been daily driving an 87 Alfa Romeo Milano for 7 years now. It's been off the road maybe 2 weeks total during that time, sometimes for optional upgrades.

I've heard endless thoughts, jokes, etc about the infamous "Alfa reliability", all from people who have never owned an Alfa. I get my laughs from my neighbor, who has often given his thoughts. He has a Toyota pickup of the same era that has driven 7000 less miles than the Alfa over our decade as neighbors due to constant issues.

2011 WRX right from the factory. Deep in the middle of the whole ring land fiasco. Here we are in 2026 and it's still standing, 170k+ later.

I bought a 2017 Dodge Durango R/T red on red. My wife liked the colors and I got V8 out of it. I traded a 2013 Nissan Pathfinder on it, that was already on a second CVT replacement under warranty. Dodge had a reputation and still does and I fully expected it to not last very long being a Chrysler product, but I bought it as a dealer demo/manager's car with about 5-6k miles in August 2017 and it now has over 203k miles. Never left me stranded. The one time it came close is when my local mechanic messed up valve cover gasket replacedment (at around 175k miles) and it started spitting oil, but that's not the car's fault. Still made it home and the shop stood behind their work and replaced it again for free. At this point, I am afraid of getting a new one, because post Covid quality went down the drain.

We bought a 2013 Fiat 500 Pop to be my daughter's first car. We got it in 2017 with 1300 miles on it.

She drove it for years, and we sold it with 87k virtually trouble free miles. About the only thing that went wrong with it was the driver's arm rest broke.

Everything else was just typical maintenance.

In my case, and without any doubts, my FIAT 500 Abarth. 14 years with absolutely no problem. none. A Fix It Again Tony they said.

My dad has now screwed around twice and gotten a secondhand CPO Maserati Quattroporte, and both have held up remarkably well. A 2015 lasted him from 2018-2025, and I'm fairly certain he made it to 100k only needing a significant brake job around the 70k mile mark. Now he's driving a '22 he bought last year. I think this one will go on a bit longer because its better in every way. The '15 felt pretty dated towards the end because, shoot, it was Chrysler interior equipment from the early 2010's. Mechanicals still seemed solid when he got rid of it.

All that said, the depreciation curve across those 7 years he drove the first was not friendly. Want to say he got in for $50k and trade it in in the high teens. Still though, you would think buying a used Maserati is a much worse decision than its turned out to be.

I bought a 100k mile '07 Quattroporte about a year and a half ago, and it has been a dream. I've put about 20k (I have a long commute) on it since then. I have had exactly ZERO issues or concerns with it. It just goes when and where I ask it to. That said, I was VERY particular when looking for one, but still got it for under $10k. The previous owner had owned it for 16 years and had all maintenance records. The only thing I've had done so far is having the bumpers and mirrors repainted to take care of the normal clearcoat issues. The backup sensors don't work, though. Maybe I'll fix that someday.

2016 GTI bought new. After dailying – and maintaining – a 944 from 2000-2013 I was deeply skeptical of another car from Ze Germans. But I observed plenty of "VW isn't like it was in the 90s" reports and risked it – knock on wood, it's been very reliable – enough so that I got a 2024 Tiguan (and a tune right off the bat) without worrying about it.

Our 2000 Porsche Boxster has given us very little issue in the 11 years we've owned it. We've replaced the water pump and the air oil separator and that's it besides regular maintenance. It regularly gets driven hard as well. Even some autox work.

1978 Fiat 124 Sport Spider. Our family has had it 20 years, we're the third owner, and I knew all of the stories but it was just so pretty and rust free. It's been a great car and it cranked and got me to work one morning when my Honda Accord wouldn't.

I bought a supercharged BMW e46 m3 sight unseen in 2019 and it's performed flawlessly as a drift car ever since. Every spring I get the oil analyized and every time the report comes back great so I keep ripping it. Rod bearing failure in these cars are over exaggerated.

DMZ key and locations in Modern Warfare 4

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

There are loads of DMZ keys in Call of Duty: Modern Warfare 4. Used to open both locked containers and locked doors, keys are your fast-track to getting your hands on some of the best loot the extraction mode has to offer. However, when you receive a key, you're only given a brief description of where to use it, so we're going to outline where you can use every key the game has to offer.

Here are all the keys we've found in Modern Warfare 4's DMZ mode and where to use them, listed in alphabetical order by the name of the key.

Note that this guide is a work-in-progress, so if the key you're looking for isn't listed here, check back soon.

The blackmarket weapons locker key can be used in two different locations in Hajin. Look for the blackmarket weapons store, identifiable by its iron wrought gates and red advert on the roof. One of them is in southern Deadtown, while the other is in eastern Hanrin Village. Go to the basement to find two lockers at each location, so if you have a second blackmarket weapons locker key, it's worth bringing with you.

Along with finding it as loot in Hajin, the coin mining den key can also be obtained by completing the "Operating Shopping Hacks" story mission. It opens a door on the third floor of an otherwise unassuming building directly north of the Netshin Tech Complex and east of the gas station found to the west of the NuriGO Mall. Inside, you'll find a number of lootable containers, including plenty of server racks, which are handy for electronic sensors.

Completing the "Operation Silent Ledger" story mission will net you the counting room vault key, but to use it, you'll need to go back into the casino. Our dedicated guide explains exactly where to go, but briefly, you'll want to take an immediate right-hand turn as you enter through the main entrance then go through the brown door in the following corridor. You'll find a bunch of safes, loose cash, and containers inside.

The executive arms auction key can be used inside the DBN Headquarters building, which is the tallest skyscraper inside The Grid. If you choose the "Operation Shopping Hacks" mission, you'll spawn nearby. This place is highly fortified, so there are loads of enemies around, and to find the locked room, you'll need to use the various elevators and ziplines dotted around to go to the very top floor (but not the roof). Here, you'll find the locked door pictured above.

The Gangbyeong apartment A key can be used on an apartment block at the northeastern end of Hajin River Heights. The building is next to the compound, and is the one that is perpendicular to the river to the south. If you need to get inside Gangbyeon apartment A without the key, then you can descend into the parking lot below and search for the staircase that will take you up inside the apartment block.

The key for Gangbyeon apartment B can usually be found inside Gangbyeon apartment A, which is quite handy. I found it on top of a table in one of the apartments. Gangbyeon apartment B is the building right next to apartment A, running parallel to the river. The key can be used to get inside either the first floor, the basement, or the roof, and if you have the militia arms depot key, you'll need to get inside apartment B first. Apartment B has several apartments to explore, full of decent loot.

Much like the blackmarket weapons locker key, there are two locations for the gun store weapons locker key, and each location has multiple lockers inside. The gun stores can be found in western Cheongun Village and northern Songdo Wharf. Each of them has an arched red brick roof and a sign above the entrance depicting a cartoon character. In the basement, you'll find multiple lockers that the keys can be used on.

The Hanex guard station key is used inside the bank within The Grid, which is the slightly smaller building next to the DBN Headquarters, which is the tallest building there. Go inside and take a zipline to the roof, then go down one floor to find the bank's vault. The locked door inside this room, pictured above, is where you can use the hanex guard station key.

The militia arms depot is located inside Gangbyeon apartment B, which is northeast of Hajin River Heights. Once you've got the key for apartment B, which can usually be found inside apartment A (details on those keys above, including how to get into apartment A without a key), you'll find the locked door for the militia arms depot on the first floor. Inside is an apartment with loads of locked containers and high-tier loot.

You'll receive the Mirae electrical room key upon completing "Operation Critical Extraction," which is the story mission where you must escort a high-value target out of the general hospital. This key unlocks a room in the underground parking lot for the hospital, which can be found by going to the northwestern corner. Enter through a door at the back and follow the signs for parking, which will lead you to a parking lot with purple walls. At the back is a room with iron bars over the windows and "danger" above the door, which can be unlocked with the key.

The police station armory key is one of the most common keys in DMZ, as it opens armories in all five police stations in Hajin. The map above shows all the locations. In each police station, the armory can be found in the basement, where you'll find weapon crates and other locked containers.

We're updating this guide with more keys as we find them, so check back soon.

Apple says there are already over 100,000 apps built for iPhone Duo

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Apple released the iPhone Duo two weeks ago, and the app story is quickly taking shape. In a new statement to 9to5Mac, Apple has revealed that there are already “well over 100,000 apps” on the App Store designed for iPhone Duo, and thousands are being added each day.

Here’s Susan Prescott, Apple’s vice president of Worldwide Developer Relations:

“It’s been wonderful to see how quickly developers have embraced iPhone Duo, and we’re grateful for their creativity and innovation. Today there are well over 100,000 apps built for iPhone Duo already live on the App Store, and thousands of optimized apps are being added to the store each day.

“In the last month alone, thousands of developers have joined us for workshops in 19 cities around the world to experience iPhone Duo firsthand and bring updated apps to their users. The enthusiasm has been incredible, and we’re excited to see the pace of updates continue to accelerate as more and more apps optimize for iPhone Duo.”

In total, Apple says that over 2,000 developers have attended more than 50 in-person workshops around the world.

Apple is continuing to spotlight apps for iPhone Duo on the App Store, including editorial curations like “Popular Apps on iPhone Duo” and “Great Games on iPhone Duo.” There’s also a “Made for iPhone Duo” badge that appears on product pages for apps optimized for Apple’s foldable.

Recent apps optimized for iPhone Duo include Microsoft Word, Microsoft Outlook, Zoom, Etsy, Halide Mark III, and more.

I detailed the iPhone Duo app experience in my full review last month. At the time, I emphasized the big difference between using an app updated for iPhone Duo and one that isn’t. I’m thrilled to see how quickly the apps I use every day are being updated for iPhone Duo.

Another trend I’m noticing is that apps that were quick to support iPhone Duo at launch have since received subsequent updates with better optimization for the inner and outer displays.

Former Rangers transfer target retires with emotional post as ex-Ibrox man returns to football

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Former England defender Conor Coady has announced his retirement - a year on from nearly joining Rangers.

Coady was a transfer target for Rangers under Russell Martin in the summer of 2025 after leaving Leicester City, but instead move to English Championship side Wrexham.

Did you know with a Digital Subscription to The Scotsman, you can get unlimited access to the website including our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

The 33-year-old started his career at Liverpool, coming through the youth ranks to make two senior appearances, before spells at Huddersfield, Wolves, Everton, Leicester, Wrexham and then a loan stint with Charlton last season.

Coady, who captained Wolves and made 317 appearances for the Molineux club, admitted in an emotional post on Instagram it was a tough decision, but which came at the right time.

“Eight clubs, 503 appearances, two promotions, a World Cup and a Euros, 10 England caps, football is my life, it always has been, but everything at some point always comes to an end,” he said.

“I never ever thought playing for Wolves in Europe or going with England to a World Cup this day would come, but honestly, the last couple of years have been tough, not playing as much as I would have liked I have struggled with, as I live for playing footie.

“The truth is I am ready now. I have given football everything and I will continue to do so in my next chapter.”

Coady won 10 senior caps for England and went to the Euro 2020 and World Cup 2022 finals.

Meanwhile, Championship strugglers Preston have signed former Rangers midfielder Greg Docherty on a deal to the end of the season.

The 30-year-old midfielder, who has previously played for Hibs among others, was a free agent after leaving Charlton in the summer and has been training with Preston for the past two weeks, playing for their under-21s in a 1-0 Premier League Cup win over Fleetwood.

Preston boss Graham Alexander, seeking to guide North End off the foot of the table, said: “We’re really pleased to bring Greg in. He’s been a captain, won promotions and knows the game inside out.

“Greg’s vocal, fit and motivated, which are all the key things you look for in a professional footballer. He has settled into the group really quickly and has a real willingness to show what he can do.”

Turns out people just really want to fully own their games

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Sometimes giving Malibu Stacy a new hat works, sometimes it doesn't. Sony is learning that offering discounts and other initiatives for PlayStation users isn't enough to make people forget about the corporation stopping physical discs come 2028.

Most lucrative is the three-month free trial for YouTube Premium. Given how that platform is now an enormous part of the world of streaming, that's a decent added incentive for signing up to PlayStation's service, on top of all the games you get immediate access to.

Introducing new perks for PlayStation Plus members. Enjoy a range of special offers, trials and discounts from PlayStation, Sony and select partners. All included with your PlayStation Plus membership. Explore the full lineup: https://t.co/EuzPbZEjlw https://t.co/rXF9FtvtXpNovember 6, 2026

But none of this stacks up to what players are losing in the coming months. This year, Sony announced it would be ceasing production of discs for new games on PlayStation consoles come January 2028.

That includes PS5 and PS4. Their libraries are becoming all digital, and fans aren't happy, because such practice raises legitimate concerns over ownership. This has led to PlayStation's replies being lit up over demand for disced games.

"The only perk I want is discs," writes one fan. "Do the trials come on disc?" asks another, incredulously. "The only perk I need to enjoy is physical discs," adds a third.

Those are just the ones for this tweet. Similar replies follow every PlayStation tweet in recent weeks.

Weekly digests, tales from the communities you love, and more

You get the point. This has become background radiation for Sony, and I don't see it dying down any time soon. The company will have accounted for pushback, but to what degree is another question. I'm not sure I see them rolling back the decision completely, but if enough people vote with their wallets in terms of cancelling PS Plus subscriptions and getting their games elsewhere, it may force some sort of change. Physical forever.

"No disc, no buy" protest and PlayStation boycott don't bother Sony, which reports increased playtime and record-high users for September.

He previously served as Senior Anime Writer at Dexerto and News Editor at The Digital Fix, on top of providing work for Variety, IGN, Den of Geek, PC Gamer, and many more. Besides Studio Ghibli, horror movies, and The Muppets, he enjoys action-RPGs, heavy metal, and pro-wrestling. He interviewed Animal once, not that he won’t stop going on about it or anything.

Diamond Foundry Announces First-Ever D200mm Single-Crystal Diamond

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Breakthrough culminates 25 years of scientific pursuit and establishes a new scale for single-crystal diamond

SAN FRANCISCO, Nov. 6, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Diamond Foundry Inc. today announced a major milestone in the history of materials technology: the creation of the first-ever D200mm single-crystal diamond, measuring 200 millimeters in diameter.

The achievement marks a fundamental step forward in humanity's ability to manufacture diamond as a technology material at semiconductor-relevant scale.

Diamond Foundry creates the world's first 200mm single-crystal diamond, bringing diamond to semiconductor wafer scale.

The announcement accompanies a keynote by Diamond Foundry CEO Martin Roscheisen, delivered by video at CarbonSemi 2026 in Ningbo, China. Watch the keynote: https://youtu.be/ae0XQBDlLMQ.

Diamond has long been recognized as an extraordinary material for technology. It combines the highest thermal conductivity with exceptional mechanical strength, electrical insulation, optical properties, and the potential for extreme electronic performance. Yet its use in advanced technology has been constrained by one basic limitation: size.

Diamond Foundry's D200mm breakthrough changes that.

Producing single-crystal diamond at D200mm scale required overcoming fundamental challenges in crystal growth, plasma physics, reactor engineering, materials science, process control, and manufacturing. The result is not an assembly of individual diamond pieces and not a polycrystalline diamond wafer. It is a single-crystal diamond at D200mm scale.

"This is one of those milestones that once seemed almost impossible," said Martin Roscheisen, CEO of Diamond Foundry Inc. "Hundreds of DF engineers, scientists, and technicians contributed to us getting here. It is the accumulated result of countless experiments, failures, inventions, rebuilt machines, and insights over more than a decade."

Roscheisen particularly recognized Stefan Gsell and Martin Fischer, whose work on scaling single-crystal diamond began 25 years ago.

"Stefan and Martin pursued this goal for 25 years," Roscheisen said. "There are very few examples in technology of someone sticking to a really hard scientific challenge with that level of perseverance. They kept going when the prevailing view was that single-crystal diamond simply could not be made at this scale. Today, the perseverance shows as the first-ever D200mm single-crystal diamond. And best of all, it makes D300mm predictable as well."

From Gemstone Dimensions to Semiconductor Dimensions

The semiconductor industry is built around the ability to manufacture extraordinary materials at wafer scale. Silicon's progression toward larger wafers transformed electronics by allowing increasingly sophisticated devices to be manufactured economically and repeatably.

Diamond has never had an equivalent scaling path.

Natural diamond is constrained by geology. Conventional lab-grown diamond has historically been constrained to relatively small crystal dimensions. Even as the extraordinary properties of diamond became increasingly relevant to high-power electronics, AI computing, optics, quantum systems, and other advanced technologies, crystal size remained a fundamental bottleneck.

The D200mm single-crystal diamond milestone demonstrates a path beyond that limitation.

For Diamond Foundry, the achievement is part of a broader effort to turn single-crystal diamond from a scarce material measured in gemstones into a tech-industrial semiconductor substrate manufactured at scale.

The implications are particularly significant as computing systems encounter increasingly severe thermal constraints. Modern AI processors concentrate enormous amounts of power into microscopic regions of silicon. Diamond's exceptional thermal conductivity can spread that heat at the material level, creating new possibilities for chip architectures, power densities, reliability, and cooling.

The D200mm achievement has roots extending well beyond Diamond Foundry itself.

For 25 years, Gsell and Fischer pursued the underlying scientific and engineering challenge of growing high-quality single-crystal diamond of scalable size. Their work ultimately became part of a much larger effort involving hundreds of people across Diamond Foundry.

The milestone reflects advances spanning plasma generation, growth chemistry, reactor architecture, crystal engineering, thermal management, metrology, automation, and manufacturing.

"Technology history is often written around the moment something finally works," Roscheisen said. "But that moment rests on the people who were willing to spend years â sometimes decades â making it work. This diamond exists because hundreds of people contributed, and because a few people refused to give up on the idea for 25 years."

Diamond Foundry views D200mm not as an endpoint, but as the beginning of a new manufacturing era for diamond.

The company is developing single-crystal diamond for semiconductor and advanced-technology applications where diamond's combination of thermal, mechanical, electrical, and optical properties can enable performance beyond the limits of conventional materials.

The first D200mm crystal is shown as grown and unpolished â directly from the growth process.

For a material that nature forms only under extraordinary conditions deep inside the Earth, the significance is simple:

Single-crystal diamond has entered the wafer-scale era.

Diamond Foundry is the #1 producer of single-crystal diamond in the world. Founded in San Francisco, the company created the world's first diamond wafer and is scaling volume production of chip substrates with a $2.77B EU Chips Act project in Spain.

Ashling's Morgan Conque Named SMB Leader of the Year Finalist at the 2026 U.S. Forces in Business Awards

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The world's largest celebration of veteran talent lands in Dallas, Texas, on December 10 to spotlight outstanding former service members driving American business forward.

CHICAGO, Nov. 6, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Morgan Conque, Vice President of Go-to-Market at Ashling, a leading global provider of AI and Automation consulting and development services, has been named a finalist for SMB Leader of the Year at the 2026 U.S. Forces in Business Awards. Now in its ninth year, the program is the world's largest celebration of military veterans in the civilian workforce, with this year's shortlist selected from hundreds of nominations.

A U.S. Army officer commissioned through ROTC, Morgan served with NATO forces in Germany, ran personnel operations in South Korea, and advised an Afghan army hospital, where she helped cut the medical evacuation cycle from 17 hours to 3. She concluded her service as the Medical Operations and Logistics Officer for the First Cavalry Division, supporting more than 18,000 soldiers.

Morgan's military experience translated into business leadership from the start. Following her service, she led branch operations for a large specialty infusion pharmacy team, then moved into intelligent automation and rapidly expanded her scope from bid manager to leading sales platforms, enablement, content, and strategy. Her education supports that trajectory, including a Bachelor of Science in Biology from Seattle University, a Master of Science in Biosecurity and Disaster Preparedness from Saint Louis University, and an Executive MBA from Baylor University's Hankamer School of Business.

Today, Morgan leads go-to-market strategy at Ashling, aligning sales, marketing, and partnership teams to drive the fast-paced growth and navigate the ever evolving market landscape. "I look back on my transition into AI and automation as serendipitous," Conque said. "The discipline I built in the Army, leading teams, making decisions with incomplete information, and staying accountable to the mission, carried directly into this field and suited me well. I remain fascinated by the conversations I'm part of and excited about where the industry is headed."

More than 200,000 service members leave active duty in the United States each year, yet research by ZipRecruiter and the Call of Duty Endowment found that almost one-third of veterans are underemployed, a rate 15.6% higher than among their non-veteran peers. Morgan's career is a testament to what that talent can achieve. Established in 2018, the Forces in Business Awards celebrate veteran role models like her, reflecting the breadth of industries and professional disciplines where former service members are making their mark.

Rear Admiral (Ret.) Milton "Jamie" Sands III, President and CEO of Special Operations Warrior Foundation, official charity partner of the U.S. Forces in Business Awards, said: "Congratulations to this year's finalists, whose achievements demonstrate the value military experience brings to business. Service develops leaders who take responsibility, build strong teams, and deliver under pressure. These finalists are putting those qualities to work in new ways, strengthening their organizations and communities while helping others see what is possible after military service."

Marshall Sied, Co-Founder and Co-CEO of Ashling, said: "Morgan's experience as a military veteran has clearly laid a strong foundation for the evolution of her career.  At Ashling, she balances our fast-paced industry and constantly evolving capabilities with enthusiasm and clarity.  Morgan has the unique ability of being a creative thinker, inspiring team leader, and strong operational executor all at once. We are honored to have Morgan help lead the charge on many of our most mission-critical initiatives. She is a very deserving finalist."

Winners will be announced on December 10, 2026, at a black-tie gala in the Lincoln Ballroom at the Hilton Dallas Lincoln Centre, hosted by longtime CBS News anchor and Gold Star son Doug Dunbar.

Ashling is a global AI and automation consulting and development firm specializing in digital process transformation. They align on what value means for enterprises, design, build and manage solutions that work from pilot to production, and architect an operating model designed for rapid deployment and compounding success.

Ashling's delivery accelerators get clients to production 5x faster and prove ROI sooner, backed by a delivery team of forward-deployed AI pods and award-winning innovation. Ashling is the firm enterprise clients trust, with over 400 clients across industries, an 86 NPS, and an unwavering commitment to the business outcomes they deliver.

The Forces in Business Awards is the world's largest business celebration of the military community. Taking place in the United States and United Kingdom, the Awards program is the leading platform for uncovering and showcasing the achievements and talents of veterans, presenting them as visible role models for transitioning service members and recognizing aspirational employers for current and former servicemen and women. The prestigious events promote the value that veterans add to the economy after service.

The Fifa hopeful hoping to take Infantino down with 'samurai spirit'

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Kozo Tashima is under no illusions it will be easy to take down Gianni Infantino - but the first serious challenger for the Fifa presidency says he will draw on his "samurai spirit".

First, the 68-year-old needs to get the endorsement of five member associations around the world.

The honorary president of the Japan Football Association said he has had "good replies" from those he has contacted since announcing his ambition to run in the March elections.

If he achieves the necessary endorsements, the next step is an altogether different proposition - toppling Infantino, who has been in charge of Fifa for a decade and told BBC Sport last month he believed he had the backing of the "vast, vast majority" of world football.

Tashima, who sits on the Fifa Council, acknowledged the scale of the challenge, but wants to bring "good change" to the organisation.

The former footballer says he is driven by disappointment in Infantino's recent actions, surrounding the cancelled suspension of United States forward Folarin Balogun at the World Cup and the abandoned plan to sell stakes in the World Cup to private investors.

In an exclusive interview with BBC Sport, he spoke of why he feels he cannot leave Fifa as it is.

"I know [Infantino] has a lot of supporters," he said. "Now I am alone and my budget is not enough but it is OK. My policy, my philosophy, I have to fight against him with my samurai spirit.

"I would like to make sure that the game of football is safeguarded and to be loved and enjoyed by everybody. We have to review the system in Fifa and build good governance.

"In the uncertain situation of the world, sports - especially football - can present good values like fair play, co-operation, hard work, integrity, and show the model of the society. So, first to review and rebuild good governance and make football accessible for all with the principle of football first."

Japan's Tashima to stand against Infantino for Fifa presidency

Fifa to share 'key documents' over World Cup sell-off plan

Infantino says he is backed by 'vast majority'

Asked how confident he was in his ability to get the five endorsements, he said: "I have confidence. I already asked to some of the member association and [received] good replies so far."

He laughed when asked if he realistically hopes to win the election in Morocco in March, before saying: "Of course, of course yes, I hope I will win. It is not easy."

Tashima's campaign will centre on his philosophy for the role of Fifa president, which he described as one based on the "values of sincerity, soundness, fair play, and integrity".

Regarding the Balogun red-card incident, which involved US President Donald Trump calling Infantino to ask for the ban to be reviewed, Tashima said: "I have never seen the red card and the next game who can play.

"This is, I hope, never happens in the world football. He didn't explain about this issue, how to come and how [it came] to happen.

He added: "Most of the things are not answered yet by Mr President Infantino.

"With my experience in Japan Football Association, Asian Football Confederation, and Fifa, for some more than 20 years, I would like to bring good change if I am elected."

Last month, former Tottenham Hotspur defender Ramon Vega announced his intention to stand as a candidate in next year's Fifa presidential election.

He told BBC Sport he had received "more than five" endorsements, but declined to give details of the identities of the member associations backing him.

All candidates must prove they have played an "active role" in football for two of the last five years. Vega has said he is confident he is eligible to stand for the presidency as a shareholder of a club.

Vega has attempted to run for the presidency three times before but each time has failed to garner the necessary support of five associations.

Tashima can therefore be considered the first serious challenger to Infantino.

Paris start-up Physicl raises $10M to make training data for physical AI

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Inovia Capital led the seed round for the start-up, whose customers include Meta, Nvidia, World Labs and Epic Games.

Physicl, a Paris-based start-up that creates training data for AI systems that work in the physical world, has raised $10M in seed funding. Inovia Capital led the round, announced on Friday, with U.S. Venture Partners also taking part.

The company focuses on what researchers call edge cases. These are the unusual situations, such as odd lighting or an unexpected object in the way, where AI models for robots, self-driving cars, and 3D vision often make mistakes.

Its first product, Studio, lets researchers rebuild those situations in 3D. They pick an environment or describe a scene where their model struggles, then change the lighting, the camera angle, and where objects sit.

Studio then produces accurate training data aimed at that specific weakness. As a result, researchers can recreate a failure, test a fix, and measure whether their model has improved.

The platform includes 10,000 3D environments and more than 100,000 objects, which can be combined into billions of different scenes, according to the company.

“Models fail on edge cases. When researchers find these weaknesses, they need a way to generate data that specifically addresses them,” said Alex de Vigan, Physicl’s founder and chief executive.

De Vigan previously founded Nfinite, a Paris company that makes 3D images of products for online retailers and raised $100m in 2022. He has spent six years working on 3D, simulation, and machine learning.

Physicl already works with AI labs and universities, including Meta, World Labs, Epic Games, Amazon Web Services, Nvidia, and the Technical University of Munich.

One customer is SpAItial, a Munich start-up that builds 3D AI models. After adding Physicl’s data to its training set, SpAItial cut the error rate of its room-building model by more than half, the company said.

Physicl will use the capital to grow its team and develop the platform further. It joins a wave of European start-ups working on physical AI, the technology that lets machines understand and act in the real world.

Before moving into senior editorial leadership, I've built my career for over +10 years across journalism, storytelling, content strategy, SEO, and digital publishing, with experience in SaaS, hospitality, art, and culture.
