Zack Snyder Pays Tribute To Late Daughter On 9th Anniversary Of Her Death

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Snyder famously left Justice League when she passed.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Filmmaker Zack Snyder is known for his unique visual style, and the stamp he put on the former DCEU (which is streaming with a HBO Max subscription). His trio of movies ended with Justice League, a movie he had to depart in the midst of shooting when his daughter Autumn died. The director/writer/producer recently took to social media to share a tribute to his late daughter on the 9th anniversary of her death.

Back in 2017 the internet exploded when Snyder departed Justice League in the midst of his daughter's death by suicide. In the years that passed he's honored Autumn with tributes, and thanking fans who helped get the Snyder Cut released. The 300 director shared a heartfelt message on the anniversary of her passing, in a post that reads:

A father’s love for his daughter never ends, even when her time here does. There are no words to describe the heartbreak of losing a child or the emptiness that lasts a lifetime. The years may pass, but the love, the memories, and the longing to have her here with us never fade.

This is a powerful message, and shows how much love remains in Snyder's family. Losing a child is something most parents cannot fathom, and reading about his ongoing feelings of loss is a powerful way of letting the public into his grief.

Zack Snyder shared this post to his 900k followers on Instagram, featuring a photo of Autumn posting with Henry Cavill in full Superman regalia. The caption encourages people to donate to the American Foundation for Suicide Prevention, while sharing more about his ongoing love for his daughter. The powerful post goes on to read:

Autumn will always be my daughter, our heroic archer. She will always be loved and always be a part of everything I do.

This is a heartbreakingly honest glimpse into Snyder's grief, and I have to assume that his words will resonate with parents who have lost children, as well as anyone who has lost a loved one to suicide. The director is keeping his daughter's memory alive, and also making a real difference in the world with his honesty and advocacy work. As he said, Autumn will be part of "everything" he does.

Fox Corp. Closes $22B Acquisition Of Roku

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Fox Corp. has closed its $22 billion acquisition of Roku, creating a massive new player in streaming.

Shares in Roku, which was founded more than two decades ago, will stop trading on the Nasdaq as of Friday.

Fox CEO Lachlan Murdoch earlier this week indicated that regulators outside the U.S. had blessed the merger, and there were reports that the U.S. Department of Justice also was nearing sign-off. But the closing came less than five months after the deal was proposed last June – well ahead of the initially projected window of the first half of 2027. Related Stories News As Roku's Merger With Fox Clears More Hurdles, Streaming Provider Delivers Strong Q3 Results Casting Katey Sagal To Recur On 'Best Medicine' For Season 2

Roku founder and CEO Anthony Wood will be involved with the combined operation, joining Fox’s board of directors and becoming Co-Chair of Fox Digital Platforms. The newly formed division includes properties like Roku, Tubi, Fox One, and Red Seat Ventures. Paul Cheesbrough is now Co-Chair and CEO of the digital division. Watch on Deadline

While Wood is staying on, plenty of questions remain about how Fox intends to structure executive teams in advertising, product and many other areas. Tubi, the free, ad-supported service Fox acquired in 2020, has thrived as a San Francisco-based group granted a degree of autonomy by its parent company.

One exec who could factor into the combination is Charlie Collier. The former CEO of Fox Entertainment has been president of Roku Media since 2022.

Ahead of the deal, Roku said its platform reaches more than 100 million households, most of them in the U.S. but also in several other countries.

The official announcement of the deal’s close said it marks the beginning of “a new transformational era” for Fox. The transaction creates “an innovative, scaled media and technology company with superior reach, engagement and monetization capabilities,” the company added.

Fox expects to realize merger-related cost savings of $400 million within two years, and also sees the deal helping its free cash flow per share by 2028.

“The closing of this acquisition advances the long-term strategy of Fox,” Murdoch said. “Beginning today, our top priority for the combined company is to continue to relentlessly serve audiences and partners, while reinforcing the openness and choice that is the essence of the Roku platform. Our shared entrepreneurial passion and clear conviction toward building the future together with our new Roku colleagues gives us great confidence in the opportunities ahead of us.”

Under the terms of the merger agreement, Roku stockholders are entitled to receive $95.30 in cash and 0.9799 shares of FOX Class A common stock for each share of Roku Class A and Roku Class B common stock they owned immediately prior to closing. As a result of the transaction, Fox stockholders own about 74% of the outstanding shares of Fox common stock and former Roku stockholders own about 26%.

Wood added in a statement, “We started Roku more than two decades ago to give people a better way to watch TV, and that mission doesn’t change today. What does change is our reach and resources. Fox has a deep respect for Roku’s culture and wholly shares our commitment to staying open to every content partner. Our teams will keep building and innovating as they always have, now with the support and capabilities of Fox behind us. I’m proud of what Roku’s employees have built, and I’m excited about what comes next.”

U.S. economy added 165,000 jobs in October, way more than expected; unemployment rate slips to 4.1%

📅 2026-11-06  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The U.S. economy added jobs at a much stronger-than-expected pace in October, while the unemployment rate moved lower as the labor market showed surprising strength.

Nonfarm payrolls increased by a seasonally adjusted 165,000 for the month, compared with the Dow Jones estimate for 59,000, the Bureau of Labor Statistics reported Friday.

The unemployment rate nudged down to 4.1%, compared with the forecast for 4.2%. A more encompassing measure that includes discouraged workers and those holding part-time jobs for economic reasons rose to 7.9%, up 0.3 percentage point, amid an increase of 421,000 in the civilian labor force.

In addition to the strong payroll gains for October, prior months were revised up substantially: The August figure moved to 186,000, an upward revision of 53,000, while the September count shifted to 60,000 following an upward revision of 31,000.

Payroll gains also came in better than forecast. Average hourly earnings increased 0.4% on the month and 3.1% annually, compared with respective estimates for 0.3% and 2.9%. The average workweek was unchanged at 34.4 hours.

"Today's jobs report is a strong rebuttal to fears that the economy is heading into a meaningful slowdown," said Seema Shah, chief global strategist at Principal Asset Management. "Payrolls growth surprised firmly to the upside, unemployment fell back to 4.1%, and job creation remains broad-based, pointing to an economy that is still absorbing labor comfortably rather than one beginning to buckle under tighter financial conditions."

The breadth of job growth also was strong, contrary to a trend in which healthcare had accounted for the bulk of gains.

Professional and business services saw an increase of 70,000 to lead all sectors, while healthcare was next at 42,000. Other areas seeing notable growth included transportation and warehousing (25,000) and manufacturing (19,000).

"The overall picture is a labor market that is on much better footing than a year ago," said Heather Long, chief economist at Navy Federal Credit Union. "In 2025, the economy added barely 10,000 jobs a month and basically all were in healthcare. This year hiring is averaging nearly 75,000 a month and there's a much more broad based range of opportunities."

The report comes the same week as an election in which the economy and in particular inflation were the center of attention. Democrats staged a nationwide rout that will give the party control of both chambers of Congress after campaigning heavily on addressing affordability issues.

For the Federal Reserve, the report also comes at a pivotal time. Central bankers are weighing whether to resume raising interest rates in December as inflation remains stubbornly above the central bank's 2% target.

Traders increased the probability of another quarter percentage point hike when the Federal Open Market Committee meets Dec. 8-9. Odds rose to 76.8%, according to the CME Group's FedWatch tool, though the indicator pointed to only one more increase after. The odds of further hikes in 2027 have fallen somewhat in recent days, after the midterm election rout and Thursday's sell-off in tech stocks.

Market reaction otherwise was muted, with a mixed picture on stock market futures heading into the open. Treasury yields were higher across the board.

The share of workers either looking for a job or already working as a share of the workforce, known as the labor force participation rate, edged higher to 61.9%. The labor force had been shrinking, the result of demographic changes and immigration reductions, but expanded by 421,000 in October.

At the same time, the household report, which is used to calculate the unemployment rate, showed 463,000 more people at work.

Long-term unemployment, though, rose sharply.

The average duration of unemployment jumped to 27.1 weeks, up 2.3 weeks from the prior month to the highest since September 2021. Those unemployed for more than 27 weeks increased to 1.96 million, the most since December 2021.

Tesla to wind down Cybertruck production in Texas next year to ramp Cybercab, Musk says

📅 2026-11-06  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Tesla CEO Elon Musk on Friday said the electric vehicle maker will "be winding down" production of the Cybertruck at its Texas gigafactory around the middle of next year.

Musk, who hailed the Cybertruck as the future of the pickup truck segment, said in a post on X early Friday morning that the end of production at the massive facility would make room for manufacturing of its Cybercab robotaxi.

The Cybertruck is exclusively produced at the company's plant in Texas alongside the Tesla Model Y and two-seat Cybercab, which Musk has touted as a crucial product for the automaker moving forward.

Musk did not say whether the company would produce the Cybertruck at a different facility, but he later responded to a user on X who called the update an "end of an era" and said the Cybertruck was the "best vehicle Tesla has ever made."

"True, but the Cybertruck fleet will be around for a long time," Musk said.

Tesla began Cybertruck deliveries in late 2023. The company did not immediately respond to a request for comment from CNBC.

The Cybertruck and its polarizing design gained a niche following, but it did not achieve mass-market sales, despite Musk's pre-launch prediction that annual sales would eventually total 250,000.

"Trucks have been the same for a very long time," Musk said when the truck was unveiled in 2019. "Like 100 years. Trucks have been basically the same. We want to try something different."

While Tesla does not break out its sales by model or region, Cox Automotive estimated sales of the Cybertruck last year at 20,237 units in the U.S., down 48% from 38,965 in 2024.

One big buyer of the Cybertruck last year was Musk's aerospace and tech conglomerate, SpaceX, which spent $131 million buying up the angular steel trucks at Tesla's suggested retail price, according to a SpaceX financial filing.

The distinct look of the vehicle made some owners a target of rude gestures and more serious road rage, one owner told CNBC last May, after Musk became the biggest funder of Donald Trump's presidential campaign and led the so-called Department of Government Efficiency, an effort to slash the federal workforce and regulations.

Wired wrote in an October 2025 feature story, "There is likely no object that feels more emblematic of US president Donald Trump's return to the White House than the Tesla Cybertruck." The magazine interviewed owners who described being spit on and sworn at due to their choice of vehicle.

The Cybertruck's specifications also fell short of those Tesla advertised early on, and in some cases fell short of those offered by leading fuel-burning pickups. There have also been many quality and production problems associated with the vehicles.

The 2024 Tesla Cybertruck was the subject of 11 voluntary recalls, according to the National Highway Traffic Safety Administration website. Issues ranged from a "trapped" acceleration pedal risk that could cause unintended acceleration, to software defects and an inverter fault that could result in sudden loss of drive power.

The Apple Watch Deals You Need to Look for This Black Friday

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

We’re expecting record-low deals in the coming weeks—but for now $150 off isn’t too bad.

It’s no secret that Apple has been dominating tech headlines this year. But between the news of its recent price hikes on MacBooks and iPads and the release of the iPhone Duo (the tech giant’s first foldable), not much fanfare has been made about the new generations of the Apple Watch. As far as we’re concerned, that’s a damn shame, because they’re well worth the upgrade—especially if you can find one on sale like we just did.

Apple Watches usually go on deepest discount during Amazon sales, Black Friday included. And while only two models are marked down right now (the Ultra 4 and Ultra 3), we know a thing or two about how Apple deals work. In the coming weeks, prices will drop and these wearables will sell out like hotcakes, which is why you’ll want to plan what you’re going to buy now.

Below, we’re breaking down the differences between each model—and peeling back the curtain on our price-tracking prowess—so you’re all set come Black Friday. If you don’t take the plunge on one before then, that is.

Commerce editor Luke Guillory argued that compared to other fitness trackers on the market, Apple was lagging behind with its lack of readiness score functionality—until now. The Apple Watch Series 12 marks the debut of the brand’s very own readiness score thanks to a revamped tracking system. Plus, the watch now measures your heart rate more frequently to deliver Heart Rate Variability (HRV) readings every five minutes. If you’re trying to pay closer attention to your health this year, it’s a smart buy, made all the sweeter when it inevitably goes back on sale for Black Friday.

For October Prime Day, this brand-new watch surprisingly dropped to just $350, so we’re expecting a similar—if not deeper—discount in the coming weeks.

While the Apple Watch Ultra 4 isn’t necessarily reinventing the wheel, it did get the same advanced health tracking and readiness score features from the Apple Watch Series 12, so if you’re looking to get your first Ultra model, it’s worth grabbing—especially at $100 off. Not to mention, Guillory is partial to the new ocean-inspired, semi-translucent rubber watch bands.

That said, if you’re not someone who needs the shiny new gadget immediately or who won’t take advantage of these souped-up health features—you’re not going to miss out on much if you score an older model on sale, like this $150 discount on the Apple Watch Ultra 3. Associate Commerce Editor Bryn Gelbart went so far as to dub it the “best Apple Watch ever” when it was released last fall.

“I was never tempted to upgrade to the premium option, but the Apple Watch Ultra 3 changed my mind,” Gelbart said. “It turns out I didn't quite know what I was missing.” It has a bright 49mm display, satellite messaging features, a durable titanium case, and a long lasting battery (an impressive 42 hours under normal use and 72 hours on Low Power Mode). Gelbart recommends it for cyclists, runners, or the adventure-prone.

At 17% off, this is the best pre-Black Friday deal to shop as of publication, although this model has dipped as low as $600 before.

If you’re looking for Apple’s most affordable watch, we suggest turning your attention to the entry-level Apple Watch SE 3. It was actually the watch that converted Gelbart to Apple wearables in the first place, arguing that its beauty is in its simplicity.

“It's an accurate and predictive workout tracker. It counts my steps and activity and connects to my phone to play music and receive messages and calls,” he says. “If you’re new to the Apple Watch and want to dip your toes, this is where I recommend you start.” It has the same sleep tracking features, like nightly sleep score and sleep apnea notifications, but lacks the ECG app or hypertension notifications of its newer siblings. It also has an 18-hour battery life so you’ll need to charge most nights.

This dropped to a record-low $199 during October Prime Day, so we’re hopeful that a similar price cut will emerge for Black Friday, but this is by far the most affordable Apple Watch on the market, even at full price.

Our philosophy when it comes to shopping for Apple tech during Black Friday? If you find the watch with the features you need at a price you can stomach, it’s worth grabbing quickly. During Prime Day, a handful of watches sold out completely—and you won’t want your wearable of choice to meet the same fate before you check out.

10 Best AI Image Generation APIs (November 2026)

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Unite.AI may receive compensation when you use links to products we review. This does not influence our editorial evaluations. Read our affiliate disclosure.

An AI image generation API brings visual creation into your own application, from product photography concepts and campaign assets to editable illustrations. Choosing one means evaluating the entire workflow: how references enter the request, how revisions work, what the output can contain and what it costs to produce an image you can actually use.

Unite.AI independently evaluated every platform in this guide for its capabilities, practical strengths, limitations and suitability for developer use cases. We considered generation and editing, output formats, integration requirements, billing and the controls needed for repeatable production. The ranking reflects editorial judgment across those needs, rather than a new hands-on speed or output-quality benchmark.

OpenAI leads as a general-purpose starting point, Google is a strong option for multimodal applications, and BytePlus Seedream is particularly relevant to reference editing and structured asset workflows. Specialist choices cover vectors, typography and enterprise brand production, while hosted catalogs provide another way to compare models. If you want a ready-made creative application, our AI art and image generators guide covers that different buying decision.

OpenAI is our leading recommendation for applications that need image generation and natural-language revision to work together. Developers can use the Image API for direct image requests or integrate generation into a broader Responses API workflow. The GPT Image family supports creating new visuals and editing supplied images, making it a strong starting point for marketing software, product-concept tools and applications that translate a customer's brief into a visual and then refine it through feedback.

Its practical strength is the connection between understanding a request and applying a change. Reference images and supported editing controls can help an application retain the intended subject while revising the background, composition or treatment. Current GPT Image 2.6 models also support markup-guided editing through the Image API. A product can turn a customer's annotation into a more focused instruction, although masks, markup and other controls must be matched to the selected endpoint and model.

Choose OpenAI when a broad creative capability and a conversational revision path matter more than access to a large collection of unrelated engines. Measure the cost using explicit quality and size settings, because text inputs, reference images and generated outputs all affect billing. API usage has its own charges and access requirements. Even a strong instruction-following model can alter fine product details or small text, so inspection remains part of turning a promising image into an approved business asset.

API billing: Text and image tokens; output settings matter.

Google's Gemini image models are a strong option when a visual feature needs to understand both a written brief and supplied media. The Gemini API exposes the Nano Banana image family, including models intended for different balances of speed, cost and asset-production quality. Generation and editing can sit inside the same multimodal application, allowing users to describe a new scene, provide a visual reference and then request further changes without opening a separate design product.

The important advantage is the range of supported workflows around the image. Selected models offer search grounding, multiple output sizes and conversational refinement, but those features should be checked at the individual model level. This suits applications that need contextual illustrations, product concepts or visuals developed through several rounds of feedback. A model with grounding support can be worth evaluating for a context-sensitive brief; it should not be treated as a guarantee that a generated factual graphic is accurate.

We recommend Google for teams already building multimodal features with Gemini or planning to combine text, images and other media. Use the current image-generation guide rather than starting a new integration from retired Imagen examples. The paid API's billing, data terms and model availability differ from the consumer Gemini application. Compare the total input and output cost at the resolution you will actually deliver, then evaluate reference consistency and text accuracy on the kinds of assets your customers request most often.

BytePlus Seedream is our recommendation for developers who want to build image creation around reference assets and deliberate revisions. Available through ModelArk, the Seedream family supports text-to-image and reference-based image generation. Seedream 5.0 Pro and Flash add interactive editing guided by positions or visual markings, along with layer decomposition. That makes the service particularly relevant to ecommerce software, campaign production and creative applications where the source product or subject matters as much as the overall style.

Those capabilities can support a more useful product experience than a prompt box alone. An application can accept a reference, let the user indicate a change and return material for further composition or editing. Layer decomposition offers another path for workflows that need elements separated after generation. It is important to match the task to the model: Pro and Flash generation behavior differs from Lite and earlier Seedream models, including the availability of sequential image generation and supported output settings.

Choose Seedream when controllable editing and reference workflows are central to the feature you are building. Evaluate the specific model's image costs, accepted inputs and regional availability before committing to a production design. API access is separate from Lumina's browser-based subscriptions, and an older plan or model name on a purchase page should not substitute for checking the actual entitlement. Reference fidelity, typography and the quality of separated layers still need review against the application's intended deliverable.

API billing: Model-specific image usage or eligible packs.

Black Forest Labs is a strong choice for developers who specifically want FLUX generation and editing through the model developer's own API. Its documentation now introduces FLUX 3 as the current family while retaining separate sections for FLUX.2 and earlier editing models. This direct route is useful when a team wants to evaluate FLUX without treating a hosting marketplace's implementation, defaults or release schedule as the full definition of the underlying model.

The API integration needs to track the requested model and its supported inputs, then retrieve the generated result through the documented workflow. That provides a foundation for product photography concepts, illustrated assets and reference-based revisions. The important engineering choice is which model to standardize on. An application that needs predictable composition or a particular edit should preserve its chosen model and parameters with each asset, rather than allowing a broad family name to stand in for a reproducible generation setup.

We recommend BFL when FLUX is already on the shortlist and direct access is preferable to a general model catalog. Compare the available image endpoints, output settings and prices with the same model on a hosting service before selecting a route. Hosted API access and any separately available model weights have different licenses and operating responsibilities. A new generation should be evaluated on the actual briefs the application serves, especially when the visual needs to preserve packaging, typography or a recognizable subject.

API billing: Model and output-dependent usage.

Ideogram is a compelling API candidate for applications producing advertisements, product graphics and other visuals where wording and controlled changes matter. Its current developer platform offers image generation and a range of editing tools, with Ideogram 4.5 positioned around targeted revisions. Precise Edit is designed to retain untouched areas while changing the requested element, and supported reference inputs can guide a product, material or visual treatment. That focus is particularly relevant when customers repeatedly revise an approved base image.

The surrounding API also includes background operations, remixing, inpainting and purpose-built commerce tools. A developer can combine these into a feature that starts with an uploaded product and ends with a set of campaign assets. The newer v2 interface identifies the content, action and model in the endpoint, while asynchronous requests and webhooks support longer operations. Model-specific availability matters: a tool listed in the wider platform should not be assumed to work with every model in the catalog.

Choose Ideogram for image applications that need readable promotional graphics and focused edits, then test those requirements with the exact wording and product references you intend to use. The platform's typography positioning is a reason to evaluate it, not a substitute for checking every delivered asset. Keep the API version and model explicit, and download successful outputs into your own storage because generated links expire. Usage pricing and concurrency also need to be matched to the application's expected production volume.

API billing: Operation and model-specific usage.

Recraft is our specialist recommendation when an application needs editable design assets as well as finished raster images. Its API supports both raster and vector generation, including SVG output, alongside editing and vectorization tools. That makes it useful for icons, illustrations, branded graphics and design systems that must remain usable at different sizes. A clean vector file can be more valuable to a production workflow than a larger bitmap that is difficult to adapt or recolor later.

The platform combines model choice with reusable styles, background operations, inpainting, outpainting and upscaling. A product can use these capabilities to develop a collection of related assets rather than treating each request as an unrelated illustration. Recraft's own documentation distinguishes model generations by their supported operations, so a newer generation model is not automatically the right choice for an editing task. Select the output format and tool according to the asset's intended destination in the design process.

Choose Recraft for graphic-design applications where vector structure and visual consistency are part of the deliverable. Inspect the generated SVG as an asset, including its shapes, text handling and suitability for the target renderer; vector output does not guarantee that a complicated design is clean or easy to revise. API credits and supported operations differ by model and output type. The strongest buying case is a workflow that benefits from design-ready formats, rather than a promise that one generator replaces every specialist illustration tool.

API billing: Credits by operation and output type.

Stability AI is a practical choice when a visual application needs several discrete image operations rather than a conversational assistant. Its platform provides Stable Image generation services, including Core and Ultra, alongside editing and upscaling capabilities. These can be combined into a pipeline that creates an image, adjusts part of it and prepares an appropriate output for delivery. The result is especially relevant to software teams that want explicit control over which operation runs at each stage.

The distinction between services is important for both creative behavior and cost. A fast generation option, a higher-detail generation and a creative upscale solve different problems. A product catalog tool, for example, may want conservative enhancement to preserve an approved item while using a more generative operation for its surrounding scene. Represent those choices clearly in the application rather than treating every endpoint as a different setting on an identical model. The desired fidelity should guide the sequence of operations.

We recommend Stability for developers who prefer a modular set of image services and want to compare the cost of each processing stage. API credits are charged by operation, so a multi-step workflow should be budgeted as a complete chain. The hosted platform is also distinct from downloading Stable Diffusion weights and running them yourself. Confirm the current endpoint, model behavior and applicable license, then inspect whether generative edits or upscaling introduce changes that are unacceptable for the source image.

API billing: Credits per service operation.

Adobe Firefly is a strong option for organizations that need image generation to fit a larger brand-production process. Firefly Services includes image generation and instruct editing, custom-model workflows and compositing APIs. Image Model 5 is the current image generation covered by its documentation. The appeal is not simply producing a striking picture: it is giving an enterprise a documented way to generate, adapt and manage visual material around its own creative assets and production requirements.

Custom models can capture a subject or style for reuse, while compositing tools place supplied products or objects into generated or selected scenes. These functions are useful to teams that need several campaigns to share a visual language. A product can incorporate an approved reference, generate a setting and then apply a controlled production step instead of rebuilding the entire image from a prompt. Each service has its own request requirements, and supported storage domains matter when supplying source assets.

Choose Adobe when brand workflows, organizational procurement and the surrounding creative stack influence the buying decision. Access to Firefly Services, model availability and contractual protections should be confirmed for the actual developer arrangement. A Creative Cloud or Firefly web subscription is not a complete API entitlement. The default organization-level rate limits also need attention before promising high-volume generation. For a small standalone application with modest requirements, a more direct self-service image API may be a simpler starting point.

API billing: Firefly Services access and usage.

fal is a useful starting point when choosing an image model is part of the product-development process. Its hosted catalog provides generation, editing and other media endpoints from different model families. This lets a team compare approaches without immediately managing its own inference hardware or opening a separate integration for every experiment. The platform is particularly appealing to a creative application that wants to offer several styles of generation while keeping account management and request handling in one place.

Its surrounding infrastructure includes queued requests, status retrieval and webhooks, which can help an application handle longer generation jobs. The model endpoint still defines the actual input fields and output behavior. A reference-editing request should therefore be designed around that endpoint's schema rather than converted blindly from another model's prompt format. Store the model route, settings and source assets with the result so that a successful image can be understood and reproduced as the product evolves.

We recommend fal for teams that value model choice and expect to keep evaluating new image capabilities. Compare its hosted price and controls with direct access to a shortlisted model, especially when one workload accounts for most of your traffic. A large catalog does not establish consistent licensing, retention, speed or feature availability across endpoints. Build the initial selection around representative briefs, then keep changes to the application's default model deliberate rather than silently replacing a proven workflow with the newest catalog addition.

API billing: Model-specific usage pricing.

Replicate is a good fit for developers who want to begin with hosted image models and retain the option to fine-tune or deploy something more specialized. Its cloud API runs models published by other developers and model companies, while its tooling also supports training and custom deployment paths. That flexibility is useful for a product that starts with general image generation but may later need a distinctive subject, a specialized style or its own preprocessing around the generation step.

The prediction workflow gives each request a tracked lifecycle, with webhooks and client libraries available for application integration. A team can compare models, preserve supported version identities and organize the associated inputs and outputs. Replicate's distinction between official and community models matters here: the publisher, license and maintenance history are part of the buying decision. A popular example that works in a notebook is not automatically the most dependable endpoint for a paid product with continuous traffic.

Choose Replicate when experimentation and a path toward customization are central requirements. Examine the selected model's pricing basis, startup behavior, file retention and deployment options before calculating production cost. Fine-tuning is available for supported models, rather than being a feature of every catalog entry. Keep a set of representative image requests for regression checks, because changing a version, a custom model or its runtime can alter the style and fidelity that users have come to expect.

API billing: Model-dependent output or compute pricing.

List the changes your users make after the first output. Reference-guided creation, masked edits, background replacement and layer decomposition are different capabilities. Verify support on the exact API model and endpoint. A feature available in a consumer editor may not be exposed through its developer API.

A per-image headline can exclude reference inputs, higher quality, larger dimensions and additional processing. Token-based services need an estimate for both input and output. Compare the cost per approved asset, including the attempts and edits needed to meet the brief. Record the model and settings so the comparison can be repeated.

A PNG, a transparent cutout, an SVG and a collection of separated layers are different deliverables. A larger raster file is not an editable vector, and a vector still needs inspection before production use. Check color, transparency, dimensions and downstream rendering with the tools that will actually consume the output.

Generated URLs often expire. Save approved assets in your own storage and keep the request identity, model, inputs and parameters with them. Confirm the throughput your account can sustain, and test the effect of model upgrades before changing a production default. Reference fidelity and readable text should be checked at the final delivery size.

Do not assume so. Developer access, billing and usage allowances are service-specific. Check the exact API account or contract, even when the same model also appears in a consumer creative application.

Recraft explicitly provides vector generation and vectorization. Other providers may expose SVG or vector workflows on selected models and endpoints. Compare whether the returned asset is a true editable vector and whether its structure suits your target design tools.

Reference images, masks and targeted editing can improve control, but exact preservation depends on the operation. Inspect packaging, logos, text, proportions and materials. Where unchanged regions must remain identical, use a workflow that explicitly preserves or composites those source pixels.

Use the same briefs, reference images, required text and delivery dimensions. Track approved-output rate, editing effort, total cost and response time. A polished sample is less informative than a repeatable result on the work your application will perform every day.

OpenAI Image API is our broad starting point for image creation and revisions. Google Gemini Image API suits multimodal applications, while BytePlus Seedream API is especially appealing when reference editing and structured asset workflows are central.

Black Forest Labs API provides direct access to FLUX. Ideogram API deserves attention for typography and targeted edits; Recraft API is the specialist choice here for vectors and reusable design styles. Stability AI API offers a modular approach to generation and image processing.

Adobe Firefly API fits brand-controlled enterprise production. Choose fal when comparing a broad hosted catalog is useful, or Replicate when experimentation and a path toward customization matter most.

Build the shortlist around the assets your users actually need to finish. The best image API for your product is the one that delivers acceptable fidelity, manageable revisions and predictable cost through the entire workflow, rather than the one with the longest model menu.

API availability and documentation checked November 5, 2026. Model access, prices and limits can change.

The AI Detours That You Shouldn’t Be Paying For

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

It’s not that frontier AI companies wouldn’t necessarily put their thumb on the scales when selling us tokens, if they could get away with it – after all, Anthropic ramped up an effective 30% price increase in June; DeepSeek raised API pricing in August; and OpenAI reluctantly walked back a downgrade in its mid-tier token allowance at the end of September. So a trend towards austerity is now pretty clear.

Indeed, that thought tends to recur when your AI agent burns through your daily token allowance in a 45-minute blaze of not-necessarily-glorious code, and tells you to come back in 3-4 hours. In such a frustrating moment, one can’t help but wonder if some of the machinations that go into that activity are weighted in the providers’ favor. After all, it’s a good way to get us to cough up for a special top-up of tokens – or even to get us to turn on ‘automatic reload’.

Well, if that ever is truly the case, in this period, the vanguard companies can very credibly hide behind the assumption that we don’t completely understand how tokens get used in agentic workloads, across models and harnesses. And neither do they. Except for their payment induction pipelines (which are very smooth indeed), it’s all pre-alpha stuff, and remains fodder for bleeding-edge research, despite the widespread commoditization of the product.

The fact that AI agents do waste tokens (and therefore waste your money) when reasoning in agentic workflows is already well-established: exactly a year ago I wrote of new research indicating the extent to which reasoning AI models proved disposed to burn tokens through ‘rambling’; earlier this year, of further research indicating chain-of-thought reasoning as ‘decorative’; and last week, Tsuga’s Head of Product Nils Bunge, in a thought-leadership piece here, declared:

‘A team that consumes twice as many tokens is not necessarily creating twice as much value. It may be handling more difficult work, using an unnecessarily powerful model, or relying on an agent that repeatedly retries failed tasks.’

What is less known is the way in which tokens get thrown away on unnecessary tasks or processes; and this is the focus of a new paper that drew my attention back to this topic today.

Titled How Do Ai Agents Waste Your Money?, this new US academic collaboration* offers a unified framework capable of quantifying how wasteful of tokens agentic processes actually are. In cases where the models are closed-source, a ‘shadow’ methodology combines with providers’ official stats and metrics to give equivalent blow-by-blows on what happened to the tokens while the AI was reasoning and acting:

The new method for measuring wasted AI tokens. Each agent’s reasoning, actions and costs are recorded, then examined for repetition, unnecessary requests and avoidable detours. Identified waste is removed, and the agent’s ability to complete the original task is tested again. Source

As shown above, the agent is allowed to work normally, intercepting and recording communications with the AI provider, including instances of reasoning, use of tools, token consumption and costs. The records are then examined by Large Language Model (LLM) judges to separate useful work from repetition, unnecessary searches and detours. After this, suspected waste is removed, the token savings calculated, and the agent’s work resumed to see whether the task can still be completed successfully.

In tests, the researchers found that around 18% of agents’ output tokens were wasted. In more than 45% of tasks, one agent wasted more tokens than another needed to complete the same job successfully. They further found that removing 41–46% of unnecessary reasoning had very little effect on performance, suggesting that substantial savings are possible without significantly compromising results.

‘Across the collected trajectories, roughly 18% of output tokens are identified as waste.

‘Strikingly, for more than 45% of tasks, the wasted output of one agent alone exceeds the entire output of another agent that successfully solves the same task.’

Additionally, the study found that agents wasted the most resources when navigating unfamiliar tools and pursuing approaches that ultimately led nowhere; and that increasing reasoning effort fails to deliver consistent improvements: at maximum effort, Kimi-K3 was found to generate 6.6 times more reasoning tokens than at low effort, without a corresponding guarantee of better results.

Test results showing how Kimi-K3 performs at four reasoning-effort levels across five benchmarks. Higher reasoning effort did not consistently improve success rates, and maximum effort sometimes produced worse results than lower settings.

By analogy, rather than driving a powerful car that one never really pushes to its capabilities, these agentic sessions are more like driving a car that will accelerate to its maximum capable speed on every trip, even when there is no benefit to it, and in spite of the possible negative implications.

The paper also examines the role that different harnesses share in creating token waste, finding that the same AI model, Kimi-K3, could generate more than four times as much reasoning on identical tasks, depending on the harness used, without necessarily achieving better results.

Some waste was traced to basic software faults, with OpenCode, Kimi Code CLI, and OpenClaw found to repeatedly launch tasks in the wrong Python environment, causing errors and token-burning retries. Kimi Code CLI also allowed the AI to request timeouts that its own software would reject, forcing yet more costly attempts.

Counterintuitively, harnesses offering more tools were not necessarily more wasteful, and some recurring inefficiencies appeared to originate in the models themselves of which harness controlled them.

‘[Several] seemingly obvious harness properties do not consistently explain the observed differences in waste. For instance, as shown [below], harnesses that expose more tools are not necessarily more wasteful. OpenClaw exposes many more tools than the other harnesses, yet exhibits only moderate waste. Moreover, not all waste changes with the harness.

‘[Below right] demonstrates that on SWE-bench Verified, there is a recurring detour in which Kimi-K3 writes out what it believes to be the project’s original fix and later abandons it. ‘

Test results comparing wasted tokens across AI agent harnesses. Left: offering more tools does not necessarily increase waste. Right: Kimi-K3 repeatedly attempts and abandons a supposed original software fix, regardless of harness, but rarely does so on a different type of task.

By way of remediation, the authors suggest that agent harnesses should recognize when environmental restrictions make further progress impossible, communicate those limitations clearly to the AI model, and stop execution rather than allowing repeated failed attempts to consume more tokens. They also propose using their measurements of wasted tokens to improve model training, reasoning strategies and harness design.

To reconstruct an AI agent’s activity, the researchers tracked each exchange between the model and the software controlling it, recording its reasoning, responses, tool requests and token costs – with the level of detail varying by provider: Kimi-K3 reveals its entire reasoning process; Gemini 3.1 Pro only summaries; and Claude exposes none.

Each tool’s results were fed back into the agent, allowing the researchers to follow the process through to completion, or until its operating limit was reached.

Four examples of how AI agents spend tokens. Repeating known information, making identical searches, and adding pointless apologies are counted as waste, while an unsuccessful search is not necessarily wasteful, since the agent may have had good reason to try it.

The agents’ requests were routed through a proxy to track their activity, with only minor code changes required, and each request tagged with its benchmark, run, task and trial (except OpenClaw, which doesn’t support such tags – in that case, a separate proxy – built on LiteLLM – was used for each test).

The study defines token waste through four recurrent patterns: Restate (repeating known information); Duplicate-Fetch (repeating unnecessary tool requests); Padding (adding irrelevant text); and Detour (pursuing plausible but ultimately unproductive approaches).

Each recorded session was examined by an LLM-as-a-Judge (LLMaJ**), which was given the original task, the complete record and criteria for identifying all four waste patterns. Suspected waste was marked and removed, and the remaining tokens recounted to calculate the wasted proportion and cost. The counts were then checked against each provider’s own tokenizers or counting tools and reported output usage (with only minor formatting differences reported).

Models tested for the study comprised Kimi-K3; Gemini 3.1 Pro; GPT-5.6 Terra; GPT-6 Astra; Claude Sonnet 5; and Claude Fable 5.1, tested over the benchmarks τ²-bench (airline and retail customer service); SWE-bench Verified (software engineering); Claw-Eval; and MCP-Atlas (general-purpose tool use).

Additionally, Kimi-K3 was tested at different reasoning levels, and five agent harnesses compared: OpenCode; OpenClaw; OpenHands; Hermes; and Kimi Code CLI – with these latter comparisons used to assess how models, tasks, reasoning effort and harness design affect token waste (and with suspected waste removed in further tests to check its effect on performance).

To establish how much money was wasted by each model, the cost of unnecessary text and tool calls was compared against total output costs, averaging the results across the four benchmarks. Internal reasoning was assessed separately for Kimi-K3 – the only model that exposed its complete reasoning process:

Test results comparing wasted output costs across six AI models, averaged over four benchmarks. Left: overall waste rates, with Kimi-K3’s internal reasoning shown separately. Right: waste rates for successful and failed tasks, with failures generally producing more waste.

As shown above, between 11% and 24.9% of the money spent generating answers and using tools was judged to have been wasted, with GPT-6 Astra performing best and Claude Sonnet 5 worst. For Kimi-K3, around one in six tokens was wasted on answers and tool use, rising to more than one in four during its internal reasoning.

More waste was generally found when tasks failed, though the reverse was true for Kimi-K3’s reasoning; and there was no clear evidence that spending more tokens on unnecessary work improved the chances of success.

In another test, an attempt was made to cut wasted tokens while the AI was still working: at each step, an AI judge** was used to remove repeated information and pointless text from the model’s reasoning, before the task was allowed to continue:

Test results showing how removing unnecessary AI reasoning affected success rates on two benchmarks. Removing 41-46% of reasoning had little effect on performance, while removing all reasoning caused substantially more failures.

On SWE-bench Verified, 41% of reasoning was cut away, with no loss of performance, with the success rate actually rising slightly, from 89% to 89.5%. On MCP-Atlas, 46% was removed, with success falling only modestly, from 65% to 62.3%.

In other words, almost half the reasoning could be discarded while the AI was working, without seriously harming its ability to finish the job.

Finally†, Kimi-K3 was tested on identical tasks using different agent harnesses. Here, reasoning output varied more than fourfold, without any consistent improvement in performance, as shown below:

Test results comparing Kimi-K3’s token consumption across agent harnesses on two benchmarks. Internal reasoning is shown in blue, and responses and tool calls in orange. On coding tasks, more than four times as much reasoning was generated with Mini-SWE as with Hermes.

Some waste was traced to basic software faults. OpenCode, Kimi Code CLI and OpenClaw launched coding tests in the wrong Python environment, causing errors and retries.

This is only a glance at a paper whose length and assiduous method may reward a deeper read.

The subject matter did bring to mind something that I am often conscious of: the sheer gulf between what we and the frontier suppliers actually understand about these processes, and the companies’ willing disposition to package the product as if it had just spent the last three months in a linter, and passed with flying colors.

As it stands, for the moment, for bad outcomes, we can currently blame no-one but ourselves, because we are buying what nearly all of us know to be a deeply experimental and unpredictable product.

* With avowedly equal contributions from University of Illinois Urbana-Champaign, Northeastern University, University of California (Los Angeles), and an independent researcher.

* The paper does not identify which model was used, as far as I can discern.

† For this article, not the paper, which is longer than I usually permit myself to cover, and contains more tests and depth than we can accommodate here. Please refer to the source PDF, linked above, for more details.
